8.3
Model-dependent exclusion limits

As no significant excess is observed in the discovery regions, upper limits are set at 95% CL for the simplified signal model for
\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}
\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\mp}_{1}
and
\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}
\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}
production with RPV decays
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}\rightarrow h\ell^{\pm}
and
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}\rightarrow h\nu
described in Section
1
. Other decay modes of the charginos and neutralinos are assumed to be negligible after selections and are not considered. The limits are determined for each mass point through signal-plus-background fits using all CRs, Run 2 and Run 3 SR1
\ell
, and Run 2 and Run 3 SR2
\ell
, with each SR2
\ell
binned in
m_{C1}
. The Run 2 and Run 3 counterparts of each region are treated as separate regions. The
\tilde{q}_{\mu}
test statistic
[
108
]
with the CL
{}_{\text{s}}
prescription
[
109
]
and the asymptotic approximation are used
[
108
]
.

Results for the hypothesis where each flavor of charged lepton is equally likely are shown in Figure
7
. SR2
\ell
provides most of the sensitivity to low chargino and neutralino masses (
\lesssim$500\text{\,}\text{G\mathrm{eV}}$
), while SR1
\ell
and SR2
\ell
have similar sensitivity to high chargino and neutralino masses.
For the branching fractions
\mathcal{B}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}\rightarrow h\ell^{\pm})=100\%
and
\mathcal{B}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}\rightarrow h\nu)=100\%
, charginos and neutralinos with masses between
150\text{\,}\text{G\mathrm{eV}}
and
1100\text{\,}\text{G\mathrm{eV}}
are excluded. This has complementary sensitivity to the previous search for a trilepton resonance
[
19
]
, which excludes charginos and neutralinos with masses between
100\text{\,}\text{G\mathrm{eV}}
and
975\text{\,}\text{G\mathrm{eV}}
for the alternate
\mathcal{B}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}\rightarrow Z\ell^{\pm})=100\%
and
\mathcal{B}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}\rightarrow Z\nu)=100\%
assumptions.

Results are also obtained for alternative flavor hypotheses. In addition to the usual SR requirements, the pure-electron (pure-muon) interpretation requires the reconstructed lepton in SR1
\ell
and two leading reconstructed leptons in SR2
\ell
to be electrons (muons). Figure
8(a)
shows the upper limits for the hypothesis where the charged lepton from the decay is always an electron while Figure
8(b)
shows the upper limits for the hypothesis where the charged lepton from the decay is always a muon. Charginos and neutralinos with masses between
150\text{\,}\text{G\mathrm{eV}}
and
1225\text{\,}\text{G\mathrm{eV}}
(
150\text{\,}\text{G\mathrm{eV}}
and
1150\text{\,}\text{G\mathrm{eV}}
) are excluded for the pure-electron (pure-muon) interpretation. Figure
9
shows the upper limits for scenarios with
\mathcal{B}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}\rightarrow h\tau^{\pm})=100\%
, excluding charginos and neutralinos with masses between
450\text{\,}\text{G\mathrm{eV}}
and
750\text{\,}\text{G\mathrm{eV}}
. The pure-electron and pure-muon interpretations have greater sensitivity than the pure-
\tau
scenario because the analysis does not reconstruct hadronically-decaying
\tau
-leptons. In addition, only part of the energy from leptonically-decaying
\tau
-leptons is retained by the daughter electron or muon, which can cause the lepton
p_{\text{T}}
to be below the
40\text{\,}\mathrm{GeV}
threshold as well as lead to misreconstruction of the chargino pairs. This also causes the pure-electron and pure-muon scenarios to have higher sensitivity than the equal electron, muon, and
\tau
-lepton scenario. The pure-electron scenario has slightly higher sensitivity than the pure-muon scenario due to a higher electron reconstruction efficiency.

\mathcal{B}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}\rightarrow h\ell^{\pm})
and
\mathcal{B}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}\rightarrow h\nu)
, which are assumed to be equal, for the hypothesis where the charginos are equally likely to decay into each flavor of charged lepton. The expected and observed limits are shown by the dashed and solid lines respectively. The shaded bands around the expected limit show the
\pm 1\sigma
variations due to statistical and systematic uncertainties, while the dotted bands around the observed limit show the
\pm 1\sigma
variations from the theoretical uncertainty on the signal cross section. The expected limits using only 1-lepton (2-lepton) regions are shown by the dashed blue and dashed red lines respectively.

and

\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}

and

production normalized to the theoretical prediction, assuming
\mathcal{B}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}\rightarrow h\tau^{\pm})=100\%
. The expected and observed limits are shown by the dashed and solid lines respectively. The shaded bands around the expected limit show the
\pm 1\sigma
variations due to statistical and systematic uncertainties, while the dotted bands around the observed limit show the
\pm 1\sigma
variations from the theoretical uncertainty on the signal cross section.

9
Conclusion

A search for
\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}
\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\mp}_{1}
and
\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}
\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}
production where each chargino and neutralino decays into a Higgs boson and a lepton was performed in a final state with one or two charged leptons and at least four jets, with at least three identified
b
-jets. This search complements a previous search for RPV chargino decays into a
Z
boson and a lepton in a channel with three or more leptons, and provides the first constraints on the simplified signal model for charginos with high decay branching fractions to Higgs bosons and leptons. The data correspond to an integrated luminosity of
196\text{\,}{\mathrm{fb}}^{-1}
of proton–proton collision data produced at center-of-mass energies of
\sqrt{s}=13

TeV
and
\sqrt{s}=13.6

TeV
, collected by the ATLAS experiment at the LHC between 2015 and 2023.

No significant excess above the SM prediction was observed. Model-independent limits are set at 95% CL on the visible cross section for new physics processes. Charginos and neutralinos with masses between
150\text{\,}\text{G\mathrm{eV}}
and
1100\text{\,}\text{G\mathrm{eV}}
are excluded at 95% CL for the
\mathcal{B}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}\rightarrow h\ell^{\pm})=100\%
and
\mathcal{B}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}\rightarrow h\nu)=100\%
hypothesis, where each flavor of charged lepton (electron, muon, and
\tau
-lepton) is equally likely to be produced. Lower limits of
1225\text{\,}\text{G\mathrm{eV}}
and
1150\text{\,}\text{G\mathrm{eV}}
are also set on the chargino and neutralino masses for the
\mathcal{B}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}\rightarrow he^{\pm})=100\%
and
\mathcal{B}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}\rightarrow h\mu^{\pm})=100\%
hypotheses respectively.
